# ANNEX 1: THE GENDER ACTION PLAN (GAP)

PNG is committed to ensure women, as well as other vulnerable groups, fully participate, engage and benefit from the National REDD+ Strategy. Similarly, following the terms of reference for the pilot programme, this section provides a comprehensive overview on the proposed action plan to ensure the RBP project, and on-the-ground activities to be financed with GCF proceeds, do not create or exacerbate gender inequalities.

Building on the findings from the GA, its recommendations, and the gender provisions in PNG's legal and institutional framework, and under the umbrella of the proposed Environmental and Social Management Framework for this RBP, this section presents the proposed Gender Action Plan (GAP). The GAP is designed to address the identified challenges, constraints, opportunities, and gaps as highlighted within this assessment by proposing gender-responsive actions to be taken during the project's implementation and implementation stages.

The GAP also proposes indicators to measure and track progress on these proposed gender action-oriented activities. This will allow for monitoring of whether which gender outcomes are being achieved during the project implementation and enable adjustments where necessary.

It is important to note that, to ensure safeguards' compliance as stated in the Project's ESMP, further assessments and plans will be conducted later on and once the specific sites for Project implementation have been identified. These assessments will also include a review of the results of the project's ESMP over the project inception. The development of such instruments would also require requests for additional funding to ensure that all stakeholders are included in these activities, including marginalized groups, such as women, youth with disabilities, customary landowners and local communities. All these processes would inform the actions of the proposed Gender Action Plan.

The action plan will also aim to identify SEARIs in stakeholder engagement, benefit sharing, and employment activities as well as to capture the commitment of the AE towards ensuring the development and implementation of clear SEARs prevention measures such as survivor-centred grievance mechanisms, development of SEAR risk mitigation plans and SEAR risk training or awareness activities.

Furthermore, the availability of gender-related data in the forestry sector, which is currently limited, as the Project's Implementation progresses, can help strengthen the GAP. At present, PNG is making efforts to collect baseline data on gender equality in the forestry sector for more accurate estimation of metrics in the future (i.e., baselines) and will significantly enhance the monitoring and evaluation framework of this project. The AE will continue to monitor the progress made and make necessary adjustments to ensure that gender equality objectives are achieved, ultimately


---
**Page 207**
---

leading to more effective and sustainable project outcomes. This type of gender-related data will be generated through the project:

- Women-to-men ratios among targeted beneficiaries in various sectors and action areas (e.g., agroforestry activities, conservation initiatives, or other sustainable livelihood options), with the aim of determining the specific number of individuals needed for skills training.
- Assessment of existing male and female capacities and gaps, focusing on key intervention areas such as sustainable land use planning/farming, conservation, sustainable forest management, and sustainable livelihood options.
- Assessment on existing male and female capacities or gaps within the key implementing agencies (e.g., knowledge of sustainable land use planning/farming options, conservation initiatives, sustainable forest management and sustainable livelihood options).
- Data on the gendered distribution of benefits before and after the implementation of the Project, disaggregated by male and female beneficiaries.
- Track changes in the receipt and distribution of benefits before and after the implementation of the Project, disaggregated by male and female beneficiaries

### C. Gender considerations integrated into planning & information systems
